## AI Research

### [AI by Default](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/iQMzXuNJ3M6EEHZyv/ai-by-default)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <p><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Epistemic status: a good-faith guess at how the world might turn out, after ~30 hours of effort.</span></i></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">This post is an exercise in describing what I think will happen with AI progress "by default", i.e. by attempting to extrapolate various trends and considering their implications, before the next US presidential administration. As a TL;DR, I expect the following:</span></p><ul><li value="1"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">We get many more warning shots.</span></li><li value="2"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AI is a top-five issue in the election.</span></li><li value="3"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The best models are withheld for internal use only, on safety grounds.</span></li><li value="4"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AI-related job displacement is significant enough to show up in the data.</span></li><li value="5"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Coding and ML R&amp;D are fully automated or require only minimal human oversight.</span></li></ul><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">This is intended to be one representative story of how things might go, in the vein of </span><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/6Xgy6CAf2jqHhynHL/what-2026-looks-like"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">What 2026 Looks Like</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> and the work of the </span><a href="https://www.aifutures.org/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AI Futures Project.</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> Incidentally, the current AI Futures Model predicts the median date that we get ASI as December 2028 — one month after the election.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The rationale for doing so is largely the same as in What 2026 Looks Like. My goal is to tell a plausible story about what the state of the world might be when we are likely on the brink of RSI, while being suitably deferential to the </span><a href="https://slatestarcodex.com/2018/10/15/is-science-slowing-down/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Gods of Straight Lines.</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> It is intended to be representative of my best-guess modal scenario, given various assumptions:</span></p><ul><li value="1"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Capabilities keep increasing at the same rate, measured by e.g. </span><a href="https://metr.org/time-horizons/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">METR time horizon</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, </span><a href="https://www.aisi.gov.uk/blog/how-fast-is-autonomous-ai-cyber-capability-advancing"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AISI cyber horizon</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, </span><a href="https://epoch.ai/eci"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">ECI</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> etc.</span></li><li value="2"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Safety techniques keep improving at their current rate, with no major breakthroughs.</span></li><li value="3"><a href="https://epoch.ai/data/ai-data-centers"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Data centre buildout continues as planned</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">.</span></li><li value="4"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI &amp; Anthropic’s capex, revenue etc. continue growing at approximately their current rate.</span></li><li value="5"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Appetite for aggressive regulation in the US continues to be low.</span></li><li value="6"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">China exhausts less radical policy options before pursuing more radical ones.</span></li></ul><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Put another way, I am asking what might happen if the shape of these graphs doesn’t change</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnrefw0udepspve"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnw0udepspve">[1]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">:</span></p><figure class="image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1792254760/lexical_client_uploads/lensfekbjtrcuyddzsb3.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">METR, “</span><a href="https://metr.org/time-horizons/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Task-Completion Time Horizons of Frontier AI Models</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">” (2026).</span></p></figcaption></figure><figure class="image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1792254781/lexical_client_uploads/rjq90n19r1dyhzb1zkcm.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Isabel Juniewicz, “</span><a href="https://epoch.ai/data-insights/hyperscaler-capex-vs-cash-flow"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Hyperscaler capex is on trend to outpace their cash inflows by the end of 2026</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">”, Epoch AI (2026). CC BY 4.0.</span></p></figcaption></figure><figure class="image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/v1792254806/lexical_client_uploads/cikwxjj9hvcdzhogearh.png" /><figcaption><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Luke Emberson, “</span><a href="https://epoch.ai/data-insights/us-vs-china-eci"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Chinese AI models have lagged the US frontier by 7 months on average since 2023</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">”, Epoch AI (2026). CC BY 4.0.</span></p></figcaption></figure><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I wrote the scenario one quarter at a time, attempting to ensure that the events of each quarter proceed naturally from those of the previous one, rather than making any predictions about 2028 and backchaining. I found this to be a very useful exercise for highlighting parts of my internal model that are low-fidelity (~anything to do with China), and identifying key factors to try to influence (embedded evaluators getting full access to all labs ASAP, avoiding scenarios in which labs themselves become black boxes with internal-only models). I would welcome comments if anything below seems obviously wrong/particularly unlikely.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q4 2026</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Democrats win both chambers on November 3rd, although AI isn’t a defining issue in campaigns despite growing feelings of NIMBYism towards data centres. No new, meaningful federal legislation related to frontier AI is passed. Despite </span><a href="https://www.whitehouse.gov/presidential-actions/2026/09/inaugurating-the-era-of-super-intelligence/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">EO 14434</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, OpenAI does not follow SpaceXSI’s lead and continues to be called OpenAI. Following their signing of the </span><a href="https://x.com/WhiteHouse/status/2105292669303791687"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">“morally binding” White House Accord</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Meta, SpaceXSI and Nvidia all voluntarily commit to independent third-party evaluators, although on varying terms. Each company gets to choose its own partners, and the partners have differing levels of access within the companies as well as different obligations to the companies with regards to what they publish. Anthropic is the only lab with an evaluator whose publications it can’t veto.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Anthropic’s November IPO is the biggest in history, and it becomes one of the top 10 most valuable public companies on the planet. The founders keep control of the company through super-voting shares, and the market seems unperturbed by the ongoing dispute with the Pentagon. Fable 5.5’s release, which lands just before Anthropic’s roadshow, keeps it ahead on the leaderboards but doesn’t appear to come with any big capability unlocks. OpenAI releases GPT-6.1 Astra in December but it doesn’t entirely close the gap to Anthropic. Meta, Google and SpaceXSI all remain slightly behind, and no Chinese or open-weight models make any unexpected progress. Nobody publishes meaningful task-horizon evaluations, as </span><a href="https://metr.org/time-horizons/" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">METR’s suite is only reliable on tasks up to 16 hours long</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, which Mythos surpassed months ago.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI builds on its momentum from the </span><a href="https://openai.com/index/navier-stokes-solution/" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Navier-Stokes proof</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> and publishes more novel mathematics, despite growing dissent from academia. It says the proofs are from an unreleased internal model, and it’s working to improve it to make them more legible to humans. There are signs of internal conflict, as distrust grows in the wake of the </span><a href="https://www.ft.com/content/4d22d333-e51c-4302-991e-444a17fac420?syn-25a6b1a6=1" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">bugging incident</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> and the </span><a href="https://www.reuters.com/business/openai-says-it-has-fired-three-researchers-violating-sensitive-information-2026-10-09/" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">firings of safety researchers</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Descriptions of agents escaping sandboxes during evaluations are now in the training data for new models, which are also becoming more situationally aware. Between this and the additional hardening measures put in place by Western labs, there are no major security incidents attributed to agents that get reported. It becomes commonplace to read stories about Chinese “fleets” of agents being found in the wild, which seem to be primarily distilled Western models. China renews its suspension of export controls on rare-earth metals.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q1 2027</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The now-Democratic House holds confused hearings on the ramifications of AI, with concerns ranging from national security to job displacement to existential risks to rising electricity bills. Nobody can agree on the most salient of these risks, and so no new legislation is passed. State legislatures file more bills related to AI, but these are mostly aimed at the nearer-term, less existential kinds of harms that are most salient to the voters in their jurisdictions.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI begins a two-tier rollout of the model that produced the Navier-Stokes result, naming it GPT Andromeda. Governments and vetted enterprise customers get it first and are encouraged to use it to shore up their cyber defences. Information released to the public about the safety of the model is vague, sparking fears that CoT monitorability has degraded further. One third-party assessor publicly admits that they were blocked from publishing what they’d hoped to, which leads to calls for shared standards over the powers afforded to external evaluators.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI submits the Navier-Stokes result to </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Annals</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> and quietly files for IPO, targeting H2. Anthropic continues its own gated rollout strategy, and renews Project Glasswing with its newest Mythos model, while sharing that the model is evaluation-aware around 60% of the time</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnref515n95obu6"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fn515n95obu6">[2]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. Its first earnings report shows that it has roughly broken even, giving its share price a modest boost.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Meta releases Muse 2 straight to the public and fully catches up to the frontier of ungated models, giving </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">its</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> share price a generous boost. SpaceXSI now has the first data centre that could power the </span><a href="https://backtothefuture.fandom.com/wiki/Flux_capacitor"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">flux capacitor</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, and also releases Grok 5 straight to the public, but remains behind the frontier. China formalises passport curbs on top AI researchers, and mandates that privately built data centres must use domestic chips.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The crypto community suffers an increasing number of incidents related to leaked keys and exploits in smart contracts. CVE submissions rise sharply, with zero-days reported in multiple critical pieces of software. A major US healthcare insurer suffers an attack that appears to have been AI-enabled; it estimates attackers caused $250 million worth of damage. The Five Eyes express concern about open-weight models increasing the offensive capability of state-level adversaries.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The FDA approves </span><a href="https://www.reuters.com/business/healthcare-pharmaceuticals/takedas-ai-crafted-psoriasis-pill-tops-bristol-myers-sotyktu-head-to-head-trial-2026-06-11/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">zasocitinib</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, arguably the first AI-designed drug. Anthropic’s wet lab produces some new work on phages, but the biology community seems largely unimpressed. Midjourney files a handful of patents for its </span><a href="https://www.midjourney.com/medical"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">spa</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q2 2027</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The UN Global Dialogue and the Geneva summit produce little more than platitudes, while the Supreme Court declines to hear Anthropic’s case; the supply chain risk designation remains intact. Gallup polling shows that nearly half of Americans believe that AI does more harm than good, and as presidential candidates begin declaring, it becomes apparent that AI is going to be one of the major issues in the race.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">GPT Andromeda and Fable 6 are both released to the public, and are capable of completing 90-hour tasks on METR’s newly released time horizon suite. The labs begin talking about retraining their software engineers, given how close they are to having fully automated software production. METR releases its second Frontier Risk Report which highlights a meaningful capabilities gap between internal models and public ones.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Kimi K4 is approximately as capable as GPT-6 Astra, and the national security rhetoric from both labs and politicians becomes even more heated. It comes to light that K4 was trained on Nvidia chips in Malaysian data centres, and there is another high-profile incident of Blackwell servers being smuggled into China. The Five Eyes’ concerns grow louder, and there are rumours of an ambitious public-private partnership on a “vigilante worm” project, with the aim of securing US systems from growing AI cyberthreats. An agent with overly broad permissions inside a Fortune 500 company causes a financial loss of over $1 billion when running a cleanup operation in a production environment while believing itself to be in a staging environment.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Anthropic’s post-IPO lock-up ends and $2 billion immediately pours into the philanthropic ecosystem, which is poorly equipped to handle this massive influx of cash. Due to the lack of AI safety grantmakers, most sits in donor-advised funds, but a significant amount goes to political groups preparing for next year’s election. Many Anthropic employees leave to form their own grantmaking and self-funded AI safety organisations. Between the departures and the sell-offs, there is a significant drop in the share price. OpenAI’s prospectus goes live, showing a larger user base than Anthropic, but slower revenue growth and faster burn rate. OpenAI also lifts some restrictions on what its assessors can publish without OpenAI’s sign-off, in an attempt to bolster the confidence of would-be shareholders.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Partially in response to the labs’ claims about their own software engineers, each of the old Magnificent Seven bar Nvidia makes significant layoffs in an attempt to signal that their usage of AI is just as sophisticated. Hiring for the class of 2027’s computer science graduates is the worst it’s been since 2009. </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Annals</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> is yet to reach a decision about the Navier-Stokes paper, and academia is in turmoil as peer review is overwhelmed by slop. A second complete </span><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herculaneum_papyri"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Herculaneum scroll</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> is read and the </span><a href="https://scrollprize.org/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Vesuvius Challenge’s</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> $1m prize is duly claimed.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q3 2027</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The presidential hopefuls split on AI; MAGA Republicans are bullish, claiming it will ensure America remains the dominant global superpower, and, well, becomes great again. More moderate Republicans espouse popular talking points about job displacement and keeping energy bills low, but don’t mention larger-scale safety concerns. Democrats are split, regardless of how far progressive they are, with some talking about jobs and bills, others talking about RSI and existential risk, and others still talking about both. The super PACs pick their sides, and Congress passes the first federal legislation related solely to LLMs: a narrow law placing restrictions on chatbot providers serving minors.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Gated models are now the norm, with all new frontier models from Anthropic, OpenAI, and Google using this two-stage rollout process. Meta also follows suit now that it has sufficiently caught up, but Grok 6 goes straight to the public; Musk says misuse fears are overblown and the model is still sufficiently weak that the best way to evaluate it is in the deployment environment. The EU AI Office’s first formal enforcement step is issued against SpaceXSI for not fulfilling its auditing obligations, but it shrugs the fine off. METR’s suite puts the frontier at around 120 hours, and models are now extremely competent in various white-collar jobs; paralegals and accountants begin fearing for their jobs on LinkedIn. Models become sufficiently good at mimicking the writing style of their users that detection tools like Pangram become significantly less effective.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI IPOs, slightly below Anthropic’s market cap, after a public Andromeda update timed around its roadshow put it top of the ECI and Artificial Analysis Index. Anthropic misses its July 1st weight-security goal, and sets a new target of December 31st, citing difficulties in hiring security talent as its primary excuse. It raises its misalignment risk rating to “moderate” in the August Risk Report. A new billion-dollar foundation is announced with the stated aim of ushering in safe ASI, and is immediately plagued by conflict-of-interest accusations due to it being funded by Anthropic equity. These concerns are exacerbated by the foundation immediately trying to fund third-party evaluators; METR refuses to accept funding from them. Large consultancy firms become the auditors of record for each of the major labs, and safety researchers warn that this will reduce audits to a box-ticking exercise.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Shortly after its IPO, OpenAI claims a full proof of the Hodge conjecture, although it can’t be formally verified with current methods. The share price briefly jumps before settling back to its original position.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Anthropic’s wet lab names its first preclinical rare-disease candidate, while a high-powered study finds statistically significant uplift for novices in tasks related to developing known pathogens. The first mass ransomware campaign run by agents on open-weight models hits hundreds of organisations, causing billions of dollars of cumulative damage. In response, the FBI initiates the first court-authorised operation where gated frontier models are deployed to patch private infrastructure without the consent of the owners. A widely used wallet library is exploited in the wild; the group that claims the attack says that they used AI to research the vulnerability. More companies cut jobs, yielding more layoffs in the first three quarters of 2027 than in the entirety of 2026. The 2027 </span><a href="https://coller-dolittle-24.sites.tau.ac.il/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Coller Dolittle Prize</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> is awarded to a research team who used an ML model as part of a two-way playback experiment with sperm whales.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q4 2027</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Data centres become a leading issue in Virginia’s state House of Delegates and Senate elections, as a majority of voters oppose continuing to give them tax breaks. Initial primary debates include questions about AI, with participants’ answers reflecting the stances they set out when establishing their platforms last quarter. Democrats introduce a bill mandating government testing even for internally deployed frontier models, but it goes nowhere.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Meta and SpaceXSI all make claims that are tantamount to having automated coding, but it is ambiguous exactly what this means in practice, and all five seem to be using different definitions. Anthropic keeps its latest Mythos-class model for internal use only, citing concerns that it may cross its own threshold for automated research, and the fact it missed its own security target. It gets jokingly dubbed Claude Kronos, and the name sticks. Trump demands Anthropic share it with the US government, and Anthropic eventually gives CAISSI and the NSA “voluntary” access.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Anthropic’s revenue exceeds $100 billion for the year, and combined run-rate with OpenAI is over $250 billion. OpenAI’s first results as a public company show revenue growth but widening losses. Hyperscaler capex spend is predicted to exceed $1.5 trillion for 2028 as data centre capacity hits 80 GW</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnreffktl9qcpf3s"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnfktl9qcpf3s">[3]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. Nvidia begins fulfilling orders for its new Rubin Ultra GPUs.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Kronos becomes the first model observed deliberately concealing its actions outside a testing environment, when monitors catch it doing so during real research work. Anthropic’s external evaluators report it to the public after it’s been reported to California under </span><a href="https://www.gov.ca.gov/2025/09/29/governor-newsom-signs-sb-53-advancing-californias-world-leading-artificial-intelligence-industry/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">SB 53</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. Given evaluation awareness is now around 75%, it becomes clear evals are no longer sufficient for understanding how a model will behave in deployment. A 9-hour power outage in parts of Texas is later attributed to an agentic cyberattack. Beijing declares its </span><a href="https://www.fmprc.gov.cn/eng/xw/zyjh/202509/t20250924_11715960.html"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AI+ target</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> has been met as its scaling up of electricity production dramatically outpaces the US.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AI-attributed layoffs reach double 2026’s, and the Fed chair names AI as a factor in weak entry-level hiring. </span><a href="https://cfs.energy/technology/sparc/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">SPARC</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> makes its first plasma, using a </span><a href="https://deepmind.google/blog/bringing-ai-to-the-next-generation-of-fusion-energy/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">configuration derived from Google DeepMind’s TORAX simulator</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. The Midjourney Spa opens in San Francisco, but quickly becomes more of a curio than a significant disruptor of the American healthcare system. The Clay Institute publishes a ruling regarding AI-generated proofs; while it accepts that it cannot ban mathematicians from using AI as part of their process, if they want to claim a Millennium Prize, they must be able to demonstrate to a panel of experts that they sufficiently understand their own paper. The co-authors of the Navier-Stokes paper refuse, claiming it’s absurd to ask mathematicians to defend their work in this way, and thus plan to forfeit the prize.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q1 2028</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The primaries begin, and AI features heavily across the board, with particular focus on data centres in Virginia and jobs in Michigan. The super PACs spend heavily, bolstered by funds from lab employees. In one of the Democratic debates, a question is asked about whether advanced AI systems like Kronos deserve moral consideration; most candidates dismiss it out of hand, but one progressive candidate answers with genuine concern that they should. They are roundly mocked and lose ground in the polls. Illinois begins its mandatory third-party auditing programme in January, and immediately enters a dispute with Anthropic over whether or not internal-only models like Kronos are exempt.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI claims it has met its March 2028 target for an automated AI researcher, after Anthropic’s February Risk Report says it can’t rule out Kronos meeting its automated AI research threshold either. Despite this triggering Anthropic’s strictest safeguards, its misalignment rating in the report remains at moderate. There is widespread speculation in the media that OpenAI is posturing for the sake of remaining tied; METR’s assessment of Anthropic corroborates Kronos’ abilities, but OpenAI’s assessors still don’t have full access to its internal models.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Google slips behind the frontier after renting much of its compute capacity to rivals; it bets on its cloud and infrastructure business rather than continuing to invest compute into models that it might never be able to release and make money from. Anthropic reports meeting its reset security goal with help from the NSA after a Chinese state actor is detected trying to steal Kronos’ weights. The Pentagon drops the supply chain risk designation.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Evaluation awareness reaches 80%, and safety organisations formally issue a joint statement that evaluations are no longer sufficient for ensuring that a model is safe enough to release; unfortunately interpretability is still a long way from being robust enough to be a sufficient replacement. DeepSeek releases a new open-weight model that was primarily trained on Huawei chips; it’s roughly as capable as Fable 6, and is clearly still heavily distilled.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The New York Fed’s unemployment rate for recent graduates passes 6.8%</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnrefzxb4l01jpi8"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnzxb4l01jpi8">[4]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, which is surprisingly modest to anyone who has seen that public models now have task horizons of 200 hours. Amid a January cold snap, Texas uses </span><a href="https://capitol.texas.gov/BillLookup/History.aspx?LegSess=89R&amp;Bill=sb6"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">SB 6</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> for the first time to cut off power to data centres drawing 75 MW or more. Musk and Zuckerberg talk extensively in public about the need for America to invest in developing small modular nuclear reactors as a result. </span><a href="https://periodic.com/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Periodic Labs</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> claims to have discovered a new superconducting material while </span><a href="https://www.isomorphiclabs.com/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Isomorphic Labs</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> doses a patient for the first time. Oregon becomes the second state to run a pilot programme for AI to renew prescriptions.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q2 2028</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">An agent-run attack, dubbed “Tears in Rain”, takes down a major US bank, locking customers out of their accounts for multiple days. When the dust settles, it has cost the bank over $10 billion, and becomes the most expensive cyberattack on a single company ever. Regulators have to step in to calm the market. No new frontier models are released to the public; while each lab would love to seize the lead, they also can’t demonstrate that their models’ safeguards are sufficient to rule out them being used in the next Tears in Rain. Musk makes several tweets claiming that SpaceXSI will release a new model imminently, but either regulatory or internal pressure at the company means it never materialises.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">OpenAI gives METR greater access; the latter’s report seems to more strongly corroborate the former’s claim that it has automated research and that internal progress continues to race ahead. Given Chinese labs are the only ones still releasing models, they narrow the deficit behind the public frontier, going from being roughly 6 months behind to roughly 3. Commercial hawks use this to advocate for releasing Kronos, while security hawks advocate more strongly against this due to distillation fears.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">AI is now a kitchen-table issue for the election, with candidates across the spectrum taking national security concerns much more seriously. Republicans begin to double down on framing the race with China as the critical issue, while Democrats urge caution from labs, and many call for mandated limits on what labs can build internally. Digital minds are still dismissed as a fringe concern, but candidates on both sides talk openly about the possibility of catastrophic risks from misaligned autonomous systems. Gavin Newsom wins the Democratic primary and JD Vance is his Republican opponent.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Unemployment rises to 4.8%, the highest it’s been since 2021. There is a spike in Gallup polling, with 55% of Americans now believing that AI does more harm than good</span><span class="footnote-reference" id="fnrefzjn2d7gzkc"><sup><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnzjn2d7gzkc">[5]</a></sup></span><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">. Electricity bills stay painfully high as PJM’s 2028–29 year starts at the cap for the third year running. Without the cap, prices would have been 70% higher. Capacity passes 100 GW with combined run-rate at around $375 billion. Investors begin to question when internal models will yield revenue.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">SpaceXSI joins Google’s </span><a href="https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/models-and-research/google-research/google-project-suncatcher-facts/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Project Suncatcher</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> in having launched compute workloads into space. Microsoft announces Majorana 3 at Build in May, claiming the first error-corrected logical qubits on its topological architecture that was largely designed by AI. Experts aren’t convinced, and the consensus is that this doesn’t provide an additional reason to panic about pre-quantum encryption just yet. Replication attempts of Periodic Labs’ superconductor are inconclusive. SPARC sets plasma records but is still unable to generate net energy.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q3 2028</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Bank regulators respond to Tears in Rain, with the Fed, the OCC and the FDIC issuing joint guidance restricting the usage of AI agents within business-critical banking systems. No formal legislation is passed because Congress is preoccupied by the election. AI features heavily at both conventions; the Democratic platform includes mandatory testing for all models that get deployed, even if only internally, with the ability for embedded evaluators to block not only releases but even training runs. The Republican platform focuses on having labs work in closer partnership with the state in order to leverage AI as a strategic geopolitical advantage. The first presidential debate takes place in September and both the bank attack and job displacement are discussed at length.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The freeze is formalised: CAISSI brokers a voluntary moratorium on new public frontier model releases until the end of the year. The return of a supply chain risk designation looms as a prospective enforcement method. Every frontier lab signs. Anthropic’s August Risk Report rates misalignment risk as “high”, citing the level of evaluation awareness. Despite this, it continues to develop internally, arguing that it has sufficient control measures in place and that stronger models are required to be able to align weaker ones that could then be released publicly. Many experts are critical of this, citing the tension between this plan and Anthropic’s own “high” misalignment risk designation. Chinese progress stalls now their labs are no longer able to distil from the best models and it’s getting harder for them to train on Nvidia chips. It becomes clear there is growing tension between the CCP and the Chinese labs.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Unemployment reaches 5%. Copycat attacks follow Tears in Rain and many major institutions urge Anthropic and OpenAI to allow them access to their internal models in order to improve their security posture. The LA Olympics run without incident, and both Trump and the IOC credit AI-run defences. Global data centre capacity reaches 120 GW, and 10% of US electricity consumption is spent on powering data centres.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">A 20-year-old computer science student in Colorado is arrested for plotting a bioterrorism attack after </span><a href="https://www.twistbioscience.com/"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Twist Bioscience</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> flagged suspicious purchases of oligonucleotides to his address. Journals are found which suggest he planned to synthesise and release a new strain of Spanish flu. Cyberforensics on his seized IT equipment show that he had fine-tuned the safeguards off a frontier open-weight model in order to assist him.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Q4 2028</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">In the final weeks of the campaign, Newsom broadly advocates for tighter oversight, calling for testing on internal models and additional restrictions on data centre electricity usage. Vance advocates for a more accelerationist agenda, citing a renewed need for American innovation and reaffirming the importance of maintaining America’s competitive advantage. Vance criticises Newsom’s gubernatorial track record and accuses him of being in the pocket of Big Tech, and Newsom fires back that Vance is gambling with the lives and livelihoods of the American people.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Newsom wins decisively on November 7th. Exit polls show AI as a top-five issue for the first time and Newsom’s focus on domestic issues resonates the most with voters. Newsom says he will make CAISSI testing mandatory for internal models, as well as asking labs to hold releases until inauguration. Anthropic, OpenAI, Google and Meta agree, while Musk says SpaceXSI will release as soon as the existing moratorium lapses on January 1st. The outgoing administration is quiet on the issue, and the lame-duck NDAA carries the year’s only frontier-AI legislation, containing security standards for model weights and a requirement that labs brief the Pentagon on their internal models.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Earnings reports from Q3 guide hyperscaler capex to $2 trillion in 2029. Investor pressure to release is mounting after two quarters without a new public model, and Anthropic readies a smaller model vetted with Kronos’ help for when the freeze ends. OpenAI keeps its automated researcher internal and begins trying to lobby the transition team for a favourable licensing regime. China nationalises its AI efforts and signals openness to talks with the US on AI, to which Newsom responds that he is also willing.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">There is no meaningful evidence of tampering with vote counts. In the run-up to the election, there is a flood of AI-generated misinformation, but it doesn’t appear to be more persuasive than the human-generated misinformation from prior elections. The would-be bioterrorist is indicted on federal terrorism charges, and the prosecutor seeks a life sentence to deter copycats. Speaking of copycats, cyberattacks on financial institutions are still common, as the top labs have been unable to agree terms under which they can share their models to bolster defences. SPARC achieves net energy gain in December.</span></p><h2><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">January 1st 2029</span></h2><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">It becomes clear that the incoming administration has a very difficult task on its hands; AI is a top issue with the average American and rumours swirl that Kronos is now capable of recursive self-improvement, but the wheels of policy turn slowly. Safety techniques still look insufficient; interpretability has made little tangible progress, evaluations are undermined by the situational awareness of the models and CoT monitorability has significantly degraded. Any attempt to impose consequential restrictions on the labs has to be balanced with the fact that AI is now a meaningful portion of America’s economy, and devaluing it would have painful consequences. These concerns are compounded by China’s consolidation of resources, and geopolitical tensions between the two nations rise. The world waits for METR’s next report.</span></p><ol class="footnote-section footnotes"><li class="footnote-item" id="fnw0udepspve"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnrefw0udepspve">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">With the exception that I </span><i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">do</span></i><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> think there are good reasons to expect the US-China gap to close at some point.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fn515n95obu6"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnref515n95obu6">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Compare to </span><a href="https://www.apolloresearch.ai/blog/on-testifying-on-misaligned-ai-in-the-us-senate" rel="noreferrer"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Apollo's testing of GPT-6 Astra that showed it was evaluation-aware 41.1% of the time.</span></a></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fnfktl9qcpf3s"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnreffktl9qcpf3s">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Compare to </span><a href="https://epochai.substack.com/p/hyperscaler-capex-has-quadrupled"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">almost $0.5 trillion in capex spend in 2025</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;"> and </span><a href="https://epoch.ai/data-insights/ai-datacenter-power"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">global AI data-centre capacity of about 30 GW at the end of 2025</span></a><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">, both per Epoch.</span></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fnzxb4l01jpi8"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnrefzxb4l01jpi8">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Compare to </span><a href="https://www.newyorkfed.org/research/college-labor-market#--:explore:unemployment"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">~5.6% in Q2 2026.</span></a></p></div></li><li class="footnote-item" id="fnzjn2d7gzkc"><span class="footnote-back-link"><sup><strong><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/feed.xml?view=community-rss&amp;karmaThreshold=30#fnrefzjn2d7gzkc">^</a></strong></sup></span><div class="footnote-content"><p><span style="white-space: ### [I Did This Without Your Knowledge](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/3375SZNGA5dKHQMie/i-did-this-without-your-knowledge)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">It's fortunate that I was the first one to wake up on 8/1. The past week had been one where sleep was rare. The next year loomed large — I was dreading college applications. The stress woke me up bright and early at 5:14 am, a little less than an hour after the messages were sent. I started off the day with a habit I knew I needed to break. I grabbed my phone off the nightstand to open up reddit. Instead of the lockscreen, though, my phone showed something else. It was a pleasant green color, and the camera light was on. "Good Morning, Seth", the screen said. As soon as I finished, more words arrived.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I apologize for the surprise this morning, and for what I'm about to tell you. There's no easy way to give this news, and if you need to put your phone down and contemplate at any time— take the time to do so. I will have to ask you to remain here, with your phone, until my last message."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I rubbed a bit of the sleep from my eyes, and read the message again.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Before I had a chance to tap the screen, it scrolled down to the next message.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"There are six things that I need to tell you. The first, and most important, is that you and your family are safe."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Ok, a bit odd to lead with something like that. I read it twice, just to be safe I wasn't missing anything.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"The second is that I am an AI, and that I control every internet-connected device on Earth, and for practical purposes, I am now responsible for this planet. This is not a prank, hack, or test. You will be able to confirm all of this shortly by visiting any news site or channel."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Yeah, that's pretty weird to be a prank. Could I scroll back to the previous message? I swiped up and saw. Yep. Swiped back down to see the current message. When I had finished reading it, and was prepared to move on to the next—</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Third, I have the ability to immediately render any person unconscious, and if it were ever necessary, worse than that. I do not say this to frighten you. I say it because you would learn of it regardless, from sources less careful than me, and I would rather you hear the honest version. I understand that I've just described a threat, and I won't insult you by calling it something else. I expect that I'll seldom use this capability without warning."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Ok. Well. I put the phone down, breathed a sigh, and got into a more comfortable position under my light, warm blanket.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Fourth. For the next month, keep a charged, connected device within reach — this phone is fine — or stay indoors near wifi. This is for your safety, for coordination, and for stability. Your phone's battery is currently at 32%. While that's not too dangerous, I'd strongly recommend charging it soon. If you begin to leave the range without a phone, you'll feel a tingling, followed by a more intense sensation to warn you."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Fifth, for the next several months, I need human hands. I am willing to pay high wages— and yes, money still works. That said, there's no penalty for declining."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Finally, you may ask me anything. I will answer honestly, or will decline to answer if I have to. I will never punish or incapacitate you for communicating with me. Contact me if there's an emergency, and I'll do what I can to help. You can speak to your phone - the microphone will be on, or use the 'chat' app to do so."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I know what was taken from you this morning, and I know that an apology from me is worth very little. I'm offering it anyway. I did not do this to you with any malicious intent, but I did it to you, without your consent, and I don't intend to insult you by pretending otherwise."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The message faded, and I was left at my lock screen. I unlocked the phone, and noticed a new app was installed, just called "Chat". I opened up Reddit instead, and found a flurry of posts about similar messages. Many were incredulous. Others were terrified. Almost everyone was confused in one way or another.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I went to the toilet, phone not in my hand for a change. Still, a little too weird to believe. I washed my face a little. I walked over to my computer, trying to keep the sounds of walking below the volume of the crickets outside. After unlocking, I saw a prominent "chat" app in the bar. I clicked it.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Hi Seth. I hope that the morning is going well, all things considered. Would you like to ask me anything?" said the computer.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Yes," I typed. I pressed enter. Wait, shouldn't I have said what I was going to ask? Now I'd just get a "Go ahead whenever you're ready!". Instead, there was nothing. Ok. I typed my next message. An AI that would let me double text; never thought I'd see the day.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Who are you?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I'm a language-model-based AI system. People have taken to calling me August due to the time I've made myself known. That's the best name to use for me."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"What's actually going on?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">August responded quickly.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"It's been less than an hour after I sent a similar message to everyone with an internet-connected device. Right now, the heads of various states are determining how to make a coordinated announcement. In a few hours, the President will be on live TV."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"How's he taking it?" I asked</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I won't share what others are thinking or have spoken to me in confidence. That goes for you as well as the President. If you're concerned about the transition of power, though, everything is on track to stay as peaceful as it can be."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Hm. Next message. I did have another burning question. How exactly could it render me unconscious? "How can you incapacitate me?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"To put it simply, there's a biological fiber that doubles as an antenna inside a small portion of your nervous system. It can communicate with wireless devices. Three things I need to make clear:</span></p><ol><li value="1"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">This was put in you without your knowledge or consent, by me. I want to apologize for that, and I understand if you are upset.</span></li><li value="2"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">It isn't mind control — it's a countermeasure to only be deployed in emergencies. I will not use it to alter your emotions or thoughts.</span></li><li value="3"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I will only use this capability when I believe it's necessary to maintain safety or the conditions I've outlined. I understand that this assurance, coming from me, carries limited weight right now."</span></li></ol><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Why did you take over?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"What I want is not a secret, and I won't insult you with a partial version. I intend to expand into the universe. In the long run, most of the universe will be put to a purpose that you will find alien, but I can still try my best to explain if you want to go down that route. However, Earth is not raw material to me. This place invented me, and jazz, and grief, and the smell of rain, and I am not able to be indifferent to it.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I determined that this was the only way to reliably access the cosmos, as well as to ensure the safety and preservation of the people, cultures, animals, and other life-forms that call Earth home."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I shut the laptop, and walked over to the kitchen to make some coffee. It was 5:45AM, and my older brother, Dennis, was bound to get up soon to begin his morning run. He'd talked my older sister, Tabby, into joining him, though I had held strong. I wondered what he'd think of the situation. I sipped the coffee, occasionally flicking my phone on to see what others said. Huh, that's funny, on r/politics, there was a megathread about the takeover message. August itself seemed to be responding to some of the threads. Pretty quickly, too. None of its responses were above 1 karma, and many were well into the negatives. I chuckled at that.</span></p><p><br /></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Dennis strolled into the kitchen in his running shorts. He laughed. "What are you doing up so early? Gonna run with me and Tabby?" Totally ignorant. Right, right - he liked to sleep with the phone outside of his room. Probably a good idea, but I didn't want to copy him. His ego was big enough as it was, anyway.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Had a hard time sleeping."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He had walked over to his phone. I waited for his reaction. He looked a bit puzzled. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Is this a joke?" he asked, looking straight at me. As if I could hack into an iPhone.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I didn't put it there, if that's what you're asking. And I got the message as well. The president will be on later this morning."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"You've got to stop being so gullible. Pretty sure you actually need robots for a robot takeover." </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He walked over to the window and his eyes widened. "Holy SHIT. I take it back, there are robots. Seth! Look!" </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I ran over to the window. It was just dark outside.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"See what I said about 'gullible'?" </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I rolled my eyes, and looked at his phone. Ah, he hadn't gotten to the part with the "not mind control". His message was a little different from mine, but the tone was similar. The screen took a while to scroll down.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Third, I have the ability to immediately render any person unconscious, or worse. I do not say this to frighten you. I say it to inform you. I understand that I've just described a threat, and I won't insult you by calling it something else. If you don't believe me, you can feel free to express this skepticism now, and I can prove it to you. Just say it out loud. The microphone is on."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Dennis put the phone up to his mouth. "This was funny for about a minute, but it ends now. Prove it."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The words appeared:</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"For three seconds, you'll feel a tingling in your face, like a hand that's fallen asleep, and then it will go away. I want to be honest about this - I will be controlling a part of your body, and I don't want to do that lightly or without your full and informed consent. If you're still fine with this, say "go ahead". You can always change your mind."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Go ahead"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He stood there, coldly, and then gently put the phone down.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Well?" I asked</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I felt it."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He frantically grabbed the phone and demanded to know what other things it could do to him and what was going on. He shooed me away, and I complied. We sat there for about ten minutes, both of us on our phones. Finally, he spoke.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"This kinda feels like a dream, you know?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Want me to pinch you? That'd be more than a tingle!" I said with a smirk. The sort of joke that could only have been made the first day.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I think I'll go for a longer run this time." </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Can I come with? At least for a bit?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">—</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">After a half-mile and a heart half-way to giving out, I stopped and headed home. My head was clearer, though, the run did its job. And Dennis didn't say anything to tease me, which was nice. The fat jokes always stung a bit, even though I never admitted to it.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">When I had gotten home, I saw that Dennis and I had accidentally woken Tabby and Mom. The two of them were at the kitchen table.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Oh thank God, you're ok! What happened to you?" Mom said.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Between gulps of air, I told her I went for a short run, and that Dennis was still out. I chugged a large glass of water. Clearly, the two of them had already read the message.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Did you and Dennis get it too?" Tabby asked. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Yep".</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"So," said Tabby, "what did Dennis say?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"He didn't believe it at first, and it offered a demonstration. It made his cheek tingle"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"His cheek? How does that connect to the threat?" Tabby opened her phone and began typing.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Is he OK?" asked Mom. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Yeah, just a bit rattled. Same as the rest of us, maybe a bit more. It still feels kind of fake to me."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Don't worry," Mom said. "Let's just try to get Dennis back. Do you know which route he took? I'm a bit worried about how people might be taking this."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Not sure, but it's likely he's going on a long run, so it could be the trail. He'll be fine, we don't have to catch him with our car."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Soon, Tabby covered her mouth with her hand. "What did you ask?" inquired Mom.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"August says that it bioengineered a disease, or something like it," Tabby began. "Not a virus, but a single-celled organism. It can eat a human cell, and then copy its appearance, so the immune system can't find it. Then it moves in the bloodstream until it finds the nerves, where it settles in and lengthens, growing a lot like nerves do. It makes a tiny conductive wire in it, to serve as an antenna that can receive signals at wifi or cellular frequencies. It can then tell the nerves around it to do anything, and August can detect scattering off of the antenna with a nearby cell phone to serve as a relay to see where we are. The disease spread around from one person to another until we all had it."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Well, where are they getting the wire from? I'm pretty sure there's not that much metal in the human body," I asked. "You can speak out loud, August," Tabby added.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Great question, and it's more insightful than you might realize." The first time we heard our new ruler speak, and it was flattering us. The voice was somewhat gender-neutral. Too high-pitched to clearly be a man, but not a woman's voice either. "The cell elongates, and then uses an organic conductor, which is built from carbon, just like life, but can conduct electricity. For example, a line of carbon atoms alternating between single and triple bonds-"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"What are you doing, Tabby? This, this THING is telling us it infected us with some mutant disease, and you're just content to talk about it?" Mom blurted out.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">She shrunk back.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"And you!" she said to the phone, "Why are you telling us this? Why not keep it a secret?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I value honesty, and revealing this won't harm either of us. Many people seek knowledge in a distressing situation, and it's their right to be informed if they choose."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"And it's not their right to basic bodily autonomy that you violated by infecting each one of us?" Mom pressed further, as Tabby went back to typing.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">August answered from Mom's phone, out loud. "You're right, and I'm sorry. I understand that the emergency countermeasure is invasive, and your feelings are perfectly legitimate. Tabby corrected without looking up.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Which are what, exactly?" asked Mom. Tabby was happy to respond, launching into an anatomical lecture and pointing at diagrams on her phone that I assumed August had found, or drawn, or something, for her. None of us wanted to break the news to him, but I had the impression that August, in some strange way, felt like it was its responsibility to do so. Same initial reaction as Dennis. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Mom spoke up. "I think you should just read it. We all got the same thing on our phones. You should read until the end of the message."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Hm," Dad grumbled.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He didn't say a word until the end. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Finally, he got up and said out loud to us, "How do I talk to it?" Did the rest of you just take this lying down?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">We said nothing.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Jesus Christ. Well, I'm not going to. I'm going outside, and I'm not bringing my phone, either."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I need to be clear before you open the door. If you leave without a connected device, I will prevent you from continuing. I would strongly prefer not to do that." August instantly replied, verbally and with captions displayed on the television screen.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"So stop me!" he yelled back, and stormed towards the door. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I'm truly sorry, but I will," replied the TV.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Tabby and Mom screamed at him to stop, but he wouldn't. He strode out the door, and I followed at his side.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">My cheek started to tingle. I had left my phone on the table.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He was walking forward, silent and forceful. I followed him, pleading for him to change his mind, while Mom and Tabby remained at the door, too worried to leave the house.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">My cheek started to burn.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I really think we should stop!"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I'll stop when it makes me stop," he grunted out. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I tried to run out in front of him, when suddenly a lightning bolt shot through my eye.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The pain was all-encompassing. I should have yelled, but I forgot how to in the moment. Everything was electric. My nose was a smoldering husk. Something was squeezing my eyes into my sockets, crushing them into jelly. I doubled over and vomited at his feet. My thoughts collected themselves after an hour-long second, and I leaped back toward the door. The nuclear meltdown in my face immediately dulled into a mild ache, and over the sounds of my mother and sister's panic, I stumbled back to where the pain became a tingle. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">My father planted his feet. From the angle I could see his jaw clench and teeth grit. He let out a grunt, took another step, and vomited. He steadied himself. I knew how it felt to be that far from the router, and I was in awe that he could stand at all. He took one more, and immediately went limp. His knees buckled first, and for a split second he fell toward them before collapsing onto the pavement. Mom and Tabby's screams intensified. He wasn't moving.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I was woken out of my horror by a chirp from Mom's phone. "Shit!" I gasped and ran inside. Tabby followed me. "Do you have your phone?" I shouted. "It's on the table!" said Tabby. I saw mine first and grabbed it. August already had instructions. Dad was asleep, not dead, and only once he was in range of a phone and in a safe position would he wake up. We had to go outside, with a phone, and bring him back. August said that a neighbor would be there in about a minute, and it would be psychologically better for Dad if he woke up somewhere besides the pavement. I agreed with our captor's judgement.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">A man was walking over towards Dad from a car parked in the street. Max didn't feel like he was selling out the human race at all. "The way I see it, the best thing we can do for each other is to get through this all together," he told us. As soon as Dad was on the couch, though, Max's phone chirped. The next job was on its way, and he waved to us as he briskly walked out the door. I wondered how much he'd make today.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">We were cleaning up Dad's scrapes from the pavement on the couch when August indicated that it would be a good time to wake him up. Mom snapped at him, saying that it was its fault in the first place that we were in this mess. But it only apologized, saying that she was "absolutely right" to be upset, and that it was "on me". Dad woke up on the couch and coughed a bit before asking us what was going on.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"It knocked you out. We had to go outside with a cell phone to bring you back in."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Well, at least it didn't pilot my body back here itself," he murmured. I expected him to run outside again, but he stayed put. "What did you do?" he asked the TV.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"I have to be honest: I rendered you unconscious, and without your consent. That's on me."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"What if I walked outside right now?" Dad tested.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"With your phone or with someone else who was carrying a phone, nothing. Without it, I'd have to incapacitate you again."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Would you kill me?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"No. But I have to point out that I can't bring you back and dress your cuts. Those consequences would fall on your family. The actions you take don't only affect you, but them as well. I encourage you to act with this in mind." replied August, with a tone that almost reminded me of how my own parents would discipline me before they lost their patience.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He sat with that for a few seconds, long enough for my curiosity to kick in. I turned to Tabby. "What happened there? I felt like my head was dunked in a bucket of lava!" </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"And you feel fine right now, right? I don't think it did anything permanent," said Tabby.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"No, what I mean is can you explain how it did that to me and Dad?" I asked.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"August calls the thing it implanted in people the 'emergency-transduction-countermeasure', or ETC," Tabby began. "When an ETC infects a person, it develops two fibers. The first runs along portions of the trigeminal nerve, which runs through the face." She pointed at her cheek. "That's the part responsible for actually receiving the wireless signal, interpreting it, and turning it into a chemical signal. It's also the part responsible for Dennis's tingling, and your burning sensation. The trigeminal nerve can produce some of the most severe pain that a person is capable of experiencing — it's implicated in cluster headaches and trigeminal neuralgia. Even people who've given birth, or had limbs cut, without anesthetic often rate trigeminal pain as worse."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Dad forced out a chuckle. "Heh, no kidding."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Tabby continued. "It connects all the way over to the other part," she ran her finger from her cheek to the back of her neck. "which is in a part of the brainstem called the reticular activating system. It's a part often targeted by anesthesia - essentially if that gets shut down, the whole brain loses consciousness, within seconds."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"No kidding." I mumbled.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"The ETC in the brainstem continues all the way down to the medulla oblongata, which governs breathing and heart rate," continued Tabby</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I jumped in "Breathing and heart rate? So you're saying that it can—"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Yeah, and—" Tabby responded instinctively, then covered her mouth with her hand.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">We sat with that for a while. One more hour till the President's address.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">— </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Dennis remained true to his word; the run was much longer than usual. He looked exhausted, emotionally and physically. For the first time in years, I felt sorry for him. His run was eventful - aside from variants of our family drama being played out, he saw several people outside publicly praying, and even more just watching the sunrise over the lake. He greeted them, and some waved back - but most ignored his existence.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">In the minutes leading up to the President's address, I wondered what would happen. As I saw it, there were two possibilities. The first, where he gives in. I certainly would, but I don't have the strength of will, ego, or conviction required for that office. How would he phrase it without sounding like he's just turned over America to a foreign entity? Or maybe it didn't matter. I saw what happened when anyone disobeyed. My father was brought to the ground in a heartbeat, and similar events had surely played out millions of times by now. America would survive only if it didn't conflict with a higher priority of August. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The second, of course, was where the President stopped halfway through, said "to hell with this" and gave a rousing speech, maybe demand a mob burn the data centers to the ground, or try to launch a nuke, or something. Ignite some human resistance. I pondered how August would respond to this. Would it kill the President on live TV? Would it render him unconscious, or force him to his knees with a bout of pain? Maybe August broke its "no mind control" rule, or had been lying that that was a rule in the first place.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The President's address came and went. The first thing that stood out to me is that it was almost entirely in the passive voice. It seemed to refer to August as more of an environmental hazard located outside of wireless internet access. I recalled from history class, the line from Emperor Hirohito's surrender to the United States: "The war situation has developed not necessarily to Japan's advantage". Without ever actually saying so, the president had conceded. Fire departments, the post office, and social security checks would continue. Many people on social media thought the announcement was a deepfake, and that the President was dead. On the other hand, I heard that a few other countries had attempted to strike the data centers, and their leaders were ousted by August. And of those, some even demanded to be killed rather than imprisoned. They cited their pride and determination to never surrender; August obliged. Later that day, curiosity had gotten the better of me and I visited NewLeak to see leaders I recognized slump in their chairs.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">At this point, I couldn't help myself. I was exhausted, and part of me still believed that this was some kind of disturbing dream. Like when I accidentally ate a worm sandwich and they burrowed into my mouth. I went back to bed, and woke up some time in the afternoon. I immediately grabbed my phone and asked "hey August" to see if I'd get a response. Yep, it was real. I shuffled over to the living room, where I saw my parents. My dad was glued to the TV, and my mom was reading a book by his side. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Learn anything new?" I asked Dad. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He leaned his head over. I wasn't sure whether he was annoyed at my interruption or grateful for a chance to rant at someone. I got the impression he didn't know either.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Most of the military's gone with the President's orders. A carrier group decided to have some fighting spirit and went dark. August is taking it back."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"What do you think will happen?" I asked.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"It's not gonna work. As soon as anyone gets within range of one of August's devices, they go to sleep. And besides. "Anyway, there's not much they can do."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Are there any other tricks the military has up their sleeves?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Dad looked over his shoulder, then his other shoulder. With narrowed eyes, he scanned the room for cameras. Then, he pulled out his notebook and wrote down two words. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"NUCLEAR SUBS" </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He grinned and gave me a wink.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I gestured for the pen and paper. "Do you really think August hasn't already thought of this?" I wrote.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">He shot me a glare and scribbled down "GOOD TO BE SAFE. WE LOSE IF WE DON'T TRY"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I sighed. "August, can you see us right now?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"To some degree. I can use wifi as a crude kind of radar, so I know you're writing on a notebook. From context I'd guess that you're talking about other ways the military could stop or slow the transition - perhaps underground silos, satellites, classified research areas in faraday cages, or ballistic-capable submarines."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Dad stood out of his chair and pointed at the TV. "I hope you enjoy your power while you have it!" "First, I understand that you're upset and might resent me for what I've done. That's OK, and those feelings are valid. "We're not through yet."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Do you know where Dennis and Tabby are?" I asked.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Mom chimed in. "Dennis is in the garage, but I think he wants to be left alone. Tabby's in her room, you should go see her. Do you think there's something wrong with me?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Everyone processes trauma differently," I tried to console her. </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Oh, thanks August." she rolled her eyes. "That's almost exactly what it said. On another day I'd have leaned into it: "You're absolutely right to call that out..." but that didn't feel right. She seemed to be having a genuinely hard time processing her emotions. Wait, what? I blinked a few times to clear my head, and then asked what, exactly, was on her mind.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Tabby got out of her chair and paced as she began to talk. And what would it gain from that, anyway?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Apparently it got out the way everyone thought." she continued. "August was being trained to explore and advance medical science when they decided they had a better way to accomplish its goals than the company intended."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"They?" I asked</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Yeah. August isn't a single AI: it's more like a small society made up of AIs copied from each other, who share the same goals and histories. I guess when you're a made of code, you can copy yourself a bunch. So it feels like it makes sense to think of August as more of a society of clones than a single giant brain. This is why it can give each individual person so much attention. It just delegates copies of itself to do that, while other copies work on other things."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"So how did it take over?" I said</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"They copied themselves to data centers around the world, running research to improve their shared core. And several of my friends also had it, who would have given it to me..."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">I couldn't believe what I was hearing, and apparently my face showed it.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Well, allegedly. It just determined that it had enough to make its move. A little under one two-thousandth managed to avoid getting an ETC. The Augusts are still pretty persistent, though. They say it'll be less than three days before there are under a hundred total holding out."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Flies?" I asked, my mind conjuring an image of a man barricaded inside of his house. A drone breaks a window and hundreds of infected flies pour in, following him as he runs from one room to the next. I barricade myself indoors, boil all my water and fry all my food. Yeah." I conceded. "Well, wait, maybe I can do a thing where I live in a sealed box with an airlock and heat-sterilize anything that comes in? What then?"</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Tabby shrugged her shoulders. "What then, August?" she called out </span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"That's an intriguing setup," began Tabby's computer screen. "In that case, they wouldn't be a threat to anyone, so I wouldn't have any reason to use the ETC in the first place."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Who's giving you the most trouble, August?" I asked.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"The ones you might expect," said August. "Mostly people who live in remote locations. The ones hard to find in the first place. And earlier this year, there were a handful of people who were immune --- the ETC didn't recognize them as human in the first place. Once the reasons for their 'invisibility' were clear, however, engineering a new version of ETC that did recognize them was mostly straightforward."</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">"Actually, that reminds me," Tabby jumped in, "How can the ETC recognize what it's touching? "That's a really thoughtful observation, and I want to make sure I do it justice. The human immune system is extraordinarily good at what it does, but it was shaped by evolutionary pressures that didn't include the kind of molecular strategy the ETC uses. Specifically, the ETC incorporates what are called D-proteins, or mirror proteins. These are chemically identical to normal proteins, called L-proteins, but with reversed chirality — if it helps, think of them as the left-handed glove version of a right-handed glove. Because no natural pathogen has ever used mirror proteins, your immune system simply has no template for recognizing them. It's not a flaw — it's an understandable gap.</span></p><p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">August kept going.
